"The pipeline had a single boolean return value that meant both 'no scanners are configured' and 'all scanners failed to run,'" Koi Security researcher Oran Simhony said  in a report shared with The Hacker News. "The caller couldn't tell the difference. So when scanners failed under load, Open VSX treated it as 'nothing to scan for' and waved the extension right through."   Early last month, the Eclipse Foundation, which maintains Open VSX, announced  plans to enforce pre-publish security checks  before VS Code extensions are published to the repository in an attempt to tackle the growing problem of malicious extensions.
